ELECTION COMMISSION OF INDIA 
ORDER 

New Delhi, the 2nd April, 1975 

S.O. 1358. — Whereas the Election Commission is satis- 
fied that Shri Arthur Tirkey, Village /P.O./P.S, -Kill anga, Dis- 
trict Sundargarh (Orissa), a contesting candidate for election 
to the Orissa Legislative Assembly from Raghunathpali(ST) 
constituency, held in Febiuary, 1974 has failed to lodge an 
account of his election expenses us required by the Rep- 
resentation of the People Act, 195], and the Rules made 
thereunder ; 

And whereas, the said candidate even after the due notice 
has not given any reason or explanation for the failure and 
the Election Commission is further satisfied that he has no 
good reason or justification; for such failure. 

Now, thcrelorc, in pursuance of section 10A of the said 
Act, the Election' Commission hereby declares the said Shri 
Arthur Tirkey to be. disqualified for being chosen as, and 
for being, a member of cither House of Parliament or of 
the Legislative Assembly or legislative Council of a State 
for a period of three years from the duie of this order. 

[No. OR-LA/ 140/74(1)]

S.O. 1359. — Whereas the Election Commission is satis- 
fied that Shri Dinabandhu Sandil, Village-Lohadarah, P.O.- 
Lathikata, District — Sundargarh (Orissa), a contesting can- 
didate for election to the Orissa Legislative Assembly from 
Raghunathpall (ST) constituency, held in February 1974 
has failed to lodge an account of his election expenses as 
required by the Representation of the People Act, 1951, and 
the Rules made thereunder ; 

And whereas, the said candidate even after the due notice 
has not given any reasoji or explanation for the failure and 
the Election Commission is further satisfied that he has no 
good reason or justification! for such failure, 

Now, therefore, in pursuance of section 10A of the said 
Act, the Election Commission hereby declares the said Shri 
Dinabandhu Sandil to be disqualified for being chosen as, 
and for being, a member of either House of Parliament or 
of the Legislative Assembly or Legislative Council of a 
State for a period of three years from the date of this 
order.

New Delhi, the 4th April, 1975 

S.O. 1350. — Whereas the Election Commission is satis- 
fied that Shri Purusottam Mohapatra, Village/P.O— Mahi- 
dhar Pada, District Cuttack (Orissa), a contesting candidate 
for election to the Orissa Legislative Assembly from Cuttack 


Sudar constituency, held in February, 1974 has failed to 
lodge an account of his election expenses as required by the 
Representation of the People Act, 1951, and the Rules made 
thereunder ; 

And whereas, the said candidate even after the due notice 
has not given any reason or explanation for the failure and 
the Election Commission is further satisfied that he has no 
good reason or justification for such failure. 

Now, therefore, in pursuance of section 10A of the said 
Act, the Election Commission hereby declares the said Shri 
Purusottam Mohapatra to be disqualified for being chosen 
as, and for being, a member of either House of Parliament 
or of the Legislative Assembly or Legislative Council of a 
State for a period of three years from the date pf this order.

New Delhi, the 5th April, 1975 

S.O. 1362.— Whereas the Election Commission is satisfied 
that Shn Mohd. Fakhruddin, Mohalla Banaulia, Bihar 
Town, Patna who was a contesting candidate for election to 
the Bihar Legislative Assembly from 195-Bihar constituency 
held in March 1972 has failed to lodge an account of his 
election expenses as required by the Representation of the 
People Act, 1951, and the Rules made thereunder; 

And whereas, the said candidate even after the due notice 
has not given any reason or explanation for the failure and 
the Election Commission is satisfied that he has no good 
reason or justification for such failure; 

Now, therefore, in pursuance of section 10A of the said 
Act, the Election Commission hereby declares the said Shri 
Mohd, Fakhruddin to be disqualified for being chosen as, 
and for being, a member of either House of Parliament or 
of the Legislative Assembly or Legislative Council of a State 
for a period of three years from the date of this order. 

[No. BR-LA/195/72(170)]

S.O, 1363. — Whereas the Election Commission is satis- 
fied that Mahanth Bhuneshwar Puri, Gosaimath, Mo- 
halla Amber, P. O. Biharsharif, Patna who was a contesting 
candidate for election to the Bihar Legislative Assembly 
from 195 Bihar consituency held in March 1972 has failed 
to lodge an account of his election expenses within in the 
time and in the manner as required by tbe Representation 
of the People Act, 1951, and the Rules made thereunder; 

And whereas, after considering the representation made by 
the said candidate, the Election Commission is satisfied that 
he has no good reason or justification for such failure ; 

Now, therefore, in pursuance of section 10A of the said 
Act, the Election Commission hereby declares the said Ma- 
hanth Bhuneshwar Puri to be disqualified for being chosen 
as, and for being, a member of cither House of Parliament 
or of the legislative Assembly or Legislative Council of a 
State for a period of three years from the date of this 
order.

S.O. 1367.-— Whereas the Election Commission is satis- 
fied that Shri Ali Sajjad Sidique, Mohalla Kohanasarai, Bi- 
harsharif, Patna who was a contesting candidate for election 
to the Bihar Legislative Assembly from 196-Asthawan cons- 
tituency held in March, 1972 has failed to lodge an account 
uf his election expenses as required by the Representation of 
Ihe People Act, 1951, and the Rules made thereunder; 

And whereas, the said candidate even after the due notice 
has not given any reason or explanation for the failure 
and the Election Commission is satisfied that he has no good 
reason or justification for such failure ; 

Now, therefore, in pursuance of section 10A of the said 
Act, the Election Commission hereby declares the said Shri 
Ali Sajjad Sidique to be disqualified for being chosen as, 
and for being, a member of either House of PaYliament or 
of the Legislative Assembly or Legislative Council of a

S.O. 1368. — Whereas the Election Commission is satis- 
fied that Shri Daleshwar Pandey, Village Majidpur, P.O. 
Jiar, Patna who was a contesting candidate lor election to 
the Bihar Legislative Assembly from 196-Asthawan consti- 
tuency held in March, 1972 has failed to lodge an account 
of his election expenses as required by the Representation 
of the People Act, 1951, and the Rules made thereunder ; 

And whereas the said candidate even after the due notice 
has not given any reason or explanation for the failure and 
the Election Commission is satisfied that he has no good 
reason or justification for such failure-; 

Now, therefore, in pursuance of section 10A of the said 
Act, the Election Commission hereby declares the said Shri 
Baleshwar Pandey to be disqualified for being chosen as, 
and for being, a member of either House of Parliament or 
of the Legislative Assembly or Legislative Council of a State 
for a period of three years from the date of this order.

S.O. 1369. — Whereas the Election Commission is satis- 
fied that Shri Ram Chandra Ram, Village & P.O. Utarthu, 
Patna who was a contesting candidate for election to the 
Bihar Legislative Assembly from 196-Asthawan constituency 
held in March, 1972 has failed to lodge an account of his 
election expenses within the time and in the manner as 
required by the Representation of the People Act, 1951, and 
the Rules made thereunder ; 


And whereas, the said candidate even after the due notice 
has not given any reason or explanation for the failure and 
the Election Commission is satisfied that he has no good 
reason or justification for such failure ; 

Now, therefore, in pursuance of section 10A of the said 
Act, the Election Commission hereby declares the said Shri 
Ram Chandra Ram to be disqualified for being chosen as, 
and for being, a member of either House of Parliament or 
of the Legislative Assembly or Legislative Council of! a State 
for a period of three years from the date of this order.

5.0. 1376. — In pursuance of clause (a) of sub-section (1)

of 10 years by the Agricultural Refinance Corporation.

Sec. 3(h)] THE GAZETTE OF INDIA ; MAY 3 1975 /VAISAKHA 13, 1897 1673 


(Department of Expenditure) 

New Delhi, the 21st April, 1975 

S.O. 1378.— In exercise of the powers conferred by 
the proviso to article 309 of the Constitution and ejause (5) 
of article 148 of the Constitution and after consultation with 
the Comptroller and Auditor-General of India in relation to 
persons serving in the Indian Audit and Accounts Depart- 
ment, the President hereby makes the following further 
amendments to the Central Civil Services (Extra-ordinary 
Pension) Rules, namely — 

1. (1) These rules may he called the Central Civil Ser- 
vices (Extra-Ordinary Pension) (Amendment) Rules, 1975. 

(2) They shall be deemed to have come into force on 
the 30th October, 1968. 

2. In the Central Civil Services (Extra-ordinary Pension) 
Rules, the existing Note below rule 10 shall be numbered 
as Note 1 and after Note 1 as so numbered, the following 
Note shall be inserted, namely : — 

“Note 2. If a Government servant who dies as a result 
of special risk of office does not leave behind 
a widow eligible for pension under these rules, his 
children shall be granted the same amount of gra- 
tuity in equal shares as would, have been admissible 
to the widow if she were alive.’’ 

EXPLANATORY MEMORANDUM 

The Central Civil Services (Extra-ordinary Pension) 

• Rules have been amended with retrospective effect 
in order to extend the benefit on the civil side 
which has already been extended to the personnel 
paid from defence estimates with effect from the 
30th October. 1968 and the interest of no one 
would be prejudicially affected by reason of the 
retrospective effect. 

[P. No. 23(13)-EV(A)/731 
S. S. L. MAEHOTRA, Under Sccy.

Office of the Dy. Chief Controller of Imports and Exports. 
ORDER 

Ahmedabad, the 14th October, 1974 

S.O. 1381. — M/s. Ashok Products, Pratap Bunglow, Fate- 
hpura, Baroda-6 has been granted licence No. P/S/1766478 
and P/S/1766479 dated 28-7-72 each for Rs. 7500/- (Rup- 
ees Seven thousand five hundred only) for import of Litho- 
graphic Zinc Plates etc. 

They have applied for duplicate copies of the said licen- 
ces (Custom purpose only) on the ground that the original 
custom purpose copies httve been lost without having been 
registered with any Custom authority. 

In support of their claim, applicant has filed an affidavit. 

I am satisfied that the custom purpose copies -of the licen- 
ce No. P/S/1766478 and P/S/1766479 dated 28-7-72 have 
been lost and direct that the duplicate of the said custom 
purpose copies of the licences should be issued to the appli- 
cant firm. 

The original Custom Purpose Copies of the licences are 
cancelled. 

[F. No. 1952/EU/1233/NP/AM.72/AU/SSI] 
D. D’SOUZA, Dy. Chief Controller

, 1 975/VAISAKHA 13, 1897 [Part II— 


ORDER 

Bangalore, the 13th March, 1975 

S.O. 1382. — M/s. Universal Textile Agency Dommasandra, 
Karnataka Stato, were granted import licence No. P/L/ 
2693796/C/XX /53/X/39. 40 P. 1.1 dated 2-12-1974 for Rs. 
1,07,335/- for import of Dyes and Chemicals etc. They 
have now applied for duplicate copy of Customs and Ex. 
change Control Purposes copy of the above licence on the 
ground that the original of the above Custom Purposes and 
Exchange Control Purposes copy of the licence have been 
lost without having been registered with any Customs Autho- 
rities and not utilised at all and that the duplicate copy of 
Customs Purposes and Exchange Control Purposes Copy of 
tile above licence now required Is for the full value of the 
licence Rs. 1,07,335/-. 

2. In support of the above contention the applicant have 
filed an affidavit. I am satisfed that the original Customs 
Purposes and Exchange Control Purposes Copy of the above 
licence have been lost and direct that a duplicate copy of 
Customs Purposes and Exchange Control Purposes copy of 
the above licence should be issued to the applicant. The 
Original Customs Purposes and Exchange Control Purposes 
copy of the above licence are hereby Cancelled. 

[No. NSF. 3/J.S. 74/REP/Bang] 
R. TAYARAM NAIDU, Dy. Chief Controller.

ORDER 

Madras, the 30th November, 1974 

S.O. 1383. — M/s. South Handys Agencies, 8, Mir Bakshi 
Ali St, Madras-14 was granted the above mentioned licence 
by this office for the Import of Coal Tar Dyes etc. 

The firm applied for grant of duplicate Exchange Control 
Copy of the above mentioned licence on the ground that the 
orginal licence was lost. It has been stated that the licence 
was not utilised by them at all. In support of their claim 
M/s. South Handys Agencies, 8, Mir Bakshi Ali Street, 
Madras- 14 have filed an affidavit. 

1 am satisfied that the original Exchange Control Copy 
of he licence has been lost and direct that a duplicate F.x- 
change Control Copy of the licence should be issued to the 
applicant. The original Exchange Control Copy of the 
licence mentioned above is cancelled.

ORDER 

New Delhi, the 16th April, 1975 

S.O. 1384. — -M/s. Bharat Heavy Plale & Vessels Ltd. Visa- 
khapatnam were granted an import licence No. l/A/1063863/ 
C/XX/4 8/H/37-38 dated 6-9-1973 for Rs. 43,00,000/- 
(Rupccs Forty-three lakhs only). They have applied for 
the issue of a duplicate Customs Purposes copy of the said 
licence on the ground that the original Customs Purposes 
only has been lost/misplaced. It is further stated that the 
original Customs Purposes copy was not registered with any 
Customs authorities unutilised fully, ft was utilised for 
Rs. Nil and the balance available on it was Rs. 43,00,000/- 
as on 2-1-1975. 

2. In support of this contention the applicant has filed 
an affidavit along with a certificate from Addl, Dist. Magis- 
trate, Visakhapatnam. I am accordingly satisfied that the ori- 
ginal Customs Purposes copy of the said licence has been 
lost. Therefore in exercise of the powers conferred under 
Sub-clause 9(cc) of the Imports (Control) Order, 1955 dated 
7-12-1955 as amended the said original Customs Purposes 
cony of licence No. I /A/1063863 /C/XX/48/H/37-38 dated 
6-9-1973 issued to M/s. Bharat Heavy Plate & Vessels Ltd, 
is hereby cancelled. 

3. A duplicate Customs Purpose copy of the said licence 
is being issued separately to the licensee, 

[No. B/7/73-74/PLS(A)]

applied for a duplicate copy of tho C.C.P. on Ihe ground that 
the original C.C.P. has been lost/misplaccd. It is further 
stated that the ordinal C.C.P. was not registered with any 
Customs House and not utilised, In support of this con- 
tention. he has filed an affidavit. I am satisfied that the 
original CCP No. P/J/3048122 duted 27-11-74 has been 
lost /misplaced and direct that a duplicate C.C.P. should be 
issued to the applicant. The original C.C.P. is cancelled.

MINISTRY OF PETROLEUM AND CHEMICALS 
(Department of Petroleum) 

New Delhi, the 16th April, 1975 


S.O. 1391 — Whereas by u notification of the Govt, of 
India in the Ministry of Petroleum and Chemicals (Depart- 
ment of Petroleum) S.O. No. 123 Dated 11-1-1975 under 
sub-section (l) of section 3 of the Petroleum Pipelines (Ac- 
quisition of Right of User in land) Act, 1962 /(5Q of 1962), 
the Central Government declared its intention to acquire the 
Right of User in the lands specified in the schedule appen- 
ded to that notification for the purpose of laying pipelines; 

And whereas, the Competent Authority has under sub- 
section (1) of section 6 of the said Act, submitted report to 
the Government ; 


And further whereas, the Central Government has after 
considering the said report, decided to acquire the right of 
user in the lands specified in the schedule appended to this 
notification ; 

Now therefore, in exercise of the Power conferred by sub- 
section (1) of the section 6 of the said Act, the Central 
Government hereby declares that the right of user in the 
said lands specified in the schedule appended to this notifi- 
cation hereby acquired for laying the pipelines ; 


And further, in exercise of the power conferred by sub- 
section (4) of that sebtion, the Central Government directs 
that the right of user in the said land shall instead of ves- 
ting in the Central Government vest on the date of the pub- 
lication of this decaration in the IndianOil Corporation 
Limited free from all cncumberances.

lands specified in the schedule appended to this notification 
hereby acquired for laying the pipelines; 

And Further in exercise of the power conferred by sub- 
section (.4) of that Section, the Central Government directs that 
the right of user in the said lands shall instead of vesting in the 
Central Government vest on this date of the publication of this 
declaration in the Oil & Natural Gas Commission free from all 
encumbrances.

New Delhi, the 21st April, 1975 

S.O. 1394. — Whereas it appears to the Central Government 
that coal is likely to be obtained from the lands mentioned 
in the Schedule hereto annexed ; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by sub- 
section (1) of section 4 of the Coal Bearing Areas (Acquisition 
and Development) Art, 1957 (20 ofl957), the Central Government 
hereby givos notice of its intention to prospect for coal therein. 

The plan of the area covered by this notification may be 
inspected in the office of the Nation .1 Coal Development Corpo- 
ration Limited (Revenue Section), Dnrbhariga House, Ranchi 
(Bihar), or in the office of the Deputy Commissioner Ranchi 
(Bihar) or in the office of the Coal Controller, 1, Count il House 
Street, Calcutta. 

Any person interested in the lands mentioned in the land 
Schedule shall deliver all maps, charts and other documents 
referred to in sub-section (7) of section 13 of the said Act to the 
Revenue Officer of the National Coal Development Corpora- 
tion Limited, Darbhanga House, Ranchi (Bihar) within ninety 
days fro ,i the date of the publication of this notification in the 
Gazette of India, as required by rule 5 of the Coal Bearing Areas 
(Acquisition and Development) Rules, 1957.

S.O, 1395. — The Central Government in exercise of the 
powers conferred on them by sub-rule (2) of rule 133 of 
Indian Electricity Rules, 1956, hereby relax provisions of 
rule 118(c), in respect of use of 240 V Supply, obtained 
from 10 KVA, 3300/415-240 V, lighting transformer in the

Kiosks, for lighting in the Donimalai Iron Ore Mine of M/s. "9 . it° ruR ATAA 

National Mineral Development Corporation Ltd., and the 

relaxation shall be subject to the following conditions: — fAfapFT, fARAT n fTTvt ALTA, fA™AT 1” 


[fro t'D 11013/ 1/ 75-t[A° ifto aTo] 
fRfl apta, frfTr 


(lj 240 Volt, system of supply shall be protected agai- 
nst earth faults by the provision of a suitable relay, 
of sufficient sensitivity to exclude the possibility of 
injury to the operators or other persons in the event 
of accidental contact. 

(2) The installation shall comply with relevant pro- 
visions of Indian Electricity Rules, 1956, particularly 
to rules in Chapter VIII. 

(3) The aforesaid relaxation shall be valid for such 
time as the Kiosks arc in use at the mine and due 
information shall be given to the Central Govern- 
ment through the Deputy Director of Mines Safety 
(Electrical), as soon as the Kiosks are taken out of 
the mine.

MINISTRY OF SHIPPING AND TRANSPORT 
(Transport Wing) 

New Delhi, the 16th April, 1975 

S.O. 1397. — The following draft of a Scheme further to 
amend the Kandla Dock Workers (Regulation of Employ- 
ment) Scheme, 1969 which the Central Government pro- 
poses to make in exercise of the powers conferred by sub- 
section ( I ) of section 4 of the Dock Workers (Regulation of 
Employment) Act, 1948 (9 of 1948), Is published as req- 
uired by the said sub-section for the information of all per- 
sons likely to be affected thereby and notice is hereby given 
that the said draft will be taken into consideration on or 
after the expiry of a period of two months from the date of 
publication of this notification in the Official Gazette. 

Any objections or suggestions which may be received 
from any person with respect to the said draft before the 
period so specified will be taken into consideration by the 
Central Government. 

DRAFT SCHEME 

1. This Scheme may be called the Kandla Dock Workers 
(Regulation of Employment) Amendment Scheme, 1975. 

2. In the Kandla Dock Workers (Regulation of Employ- 
ment) Scheme, 1969, in item (c) of sub-clause (1) of clause 
16 for the words “and in case”, the words “and in no case” 
shall be substituted. 

[F. No. 57/4/70-P&D/LD] 
V. SANKARAI.INGAM, Under Secy.

New Delhi, the 23rd April, 1975 

S.O. 1402. — In pursuance of section 17 of the Industrial 
Disputes Act. 1947 (14 of 1947), the Central Government 
hereby publishes the following award of the Central Govern- 
ment Industrial Tribunal. Calcutta in the industrial dispute 
between (he employers in relation to the management of 
Messrs Modern Carpentury Works, Calcutta and their 
workmen, which was received by the Central Government 
on the I9lh April, 1975. 

CENTRAL GOVERNMENT INDUSTRIAL TRIBUNAL 
AT CALCUTTA 
Reference! No, 13 of 1974 


PARTIES : 


Employees in relation to the management of Messrs 
Modern Carpentary Works, Calcutta, 

AND 

Their Workmen 

APPEARANCES ; 

On behalf of Employers — Sri Biswanath Ash, Advocate. 

On behalf of Workmen — Sri H. L. Roy, Advocate. 

State : West Bengal Industry ; Port & Dock 

AWARD 

The Ministry of Labour, Government of India, by Its 
Order No. L3201 1 /5/74-P&D/CMT dated 23rd September, 
1974, referred an industrial dispute to this tribunal for ad- 
judication which reads as follows ; 

"Whether the demand for increase in wages of Mistries, 
Carpenters and helpers is justified ? If so, at what 
rate and with what other details 7" 

2. The union of workmen in their written statement 
raised the contention that the workmen are entitled to an 
increased wage from Rs. 8, 9, 10 to Rs. 10/- for the first 
shift and from Rs. 9, 10 and 11/- to Rs. 14/- for the sec- 
ond and third shifts as according to them the cost of living 
index has enormously increased over a course of years and 
there are circumstances justifying an upward revision of 
wages in the concern. They have also claimed dearness all- 
owance over the existing rates of wages to neutralise the 
rise in cost of living. 

3. In answer to the claim the company in question stated 
that the claim of increased wages is not justified either for 
the first shift or for other shifts; that the claim was res- 
tricted only two basic wage before the conciliation officer. The 
workmen, therefore, cannot go back upon the case which 
they set up before the Conciliation Officer and make an 
additional claim for dearness allowance. They dispute the 
workmen's claim for enhanced wages. 

4. When the matter came up for hearing before me, the 
parlies entered into a compromise. A signed compromise 
was filed before the tribunal with a prayer to pass an award 
In terms of the compromise. The copy of compromise 
petition is annexed with the award and an award is passed 
in terms of the compromise. 

The reference is answered accordingly. 

E, K. MOIDU, Presiding Officer 
[No. L-32011/5/74-P&D/CMT/D IV(n)] 
NAND LAL, Section Officer (Special) 

Dated, Calcutta, 

The 11th Aprl, 1975. 

BEFORE THE CENTRAL GOVERNMENT INDUSTRIAL 
TRIBUNAL 20, BRITISH INDIAN STREET (1st FLOOR), 
CALCUTTA 

Reference Not 13 of 1974 
Tn the matter of an Industrial Dispute 
BETWEEN 

Employers in relation to the Management of Modern 
Carpentary Works, 

P-21, Kahikar Street, Calcutta-?. 

AND 

Their Workmen, represented by Registered & Un- 
registered Dock Workers’ Union, 130, Circular 
Garden Reach Road, Kiddcrporc, Calcutta-23. 

The humble joint petition on behalf of the parties. 
MOST RESPECTFULLY WHEWETII ; 

(1) That the dispute was fixed for further hearing to-day. 

(2) That the dispute as per schedule to the order of 
Reference, namely; 



1719 


Sec. 3(ii)] THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : MAY 3, 1975/VAISAKHA 13, 1397 


“Whether the demand for increase in wages of Mis- 
tries, Carpenters and helpers is justified 7 Tf so, 
at what rate and with what other details ?" 


has been amicably settled out of the Tribunal, by parties, 
on the following terms, namely. 

(1) that the workmen as per list of the union’s Writlen 
Statement will be allowed increment at the flat rate 
of Re l/- per shift with retrospective effect from 
23rd September, 1974. 

(2) That this settles the present dispute under this order 

of Reference completely. 

It is humbly prayed that your Honour would be pleased 
to make an Award incorporating the settlement and 
in terms thereof. 


And, lor this act of kindness, as in duty bounds, your 
petitioners shall fiver pray.

S.O. 1414 — Whereas the Central Government being satis- 
fied that the public interest so required, had declared by a 
notification made in pursuance of the provision of the pro- 
viso to sub-clause (vi) of clause (n) of section 2 of the In- 
dustrial Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947), being the notifica- 
tion of (he Government of India in the Ministry of Labour 
No. S.O, 3053 dated the 1st November, 1974 service in any 
oil-field, to be a public utility service for the purpose of the 
said Act for a period of six months from the 22nd Novem- 
ber, 1974; 

And whereas the Central Government is of opinion that 
public interest requires the extension of the said period by 
a further period of six months ; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by 
the proviso to sub-clause (vi) of clause (n) of section 2 of 
the Indutrial Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947), the Central 
Government hereby declares the said service to be a public 
utility service for the purposes of the said Act for a further 
period of six months from the 22nd May, 1975.

S.O. 1415.— In exercise of the powers conferred by rule 31 
of the Iron Ore Mines Labour Welfare Cess Rules, 1963, 
the Central Government hereby specifies the Welfare Com- 
missioners, Iron Ore Mines Welfare Organisations in the 
States of Bihar & Orissa, Karnataka, Maharashtra and Andhra 
Pradesh and the LTnion Territory of Goa to be Iron Ore Mines 
Cess Commissioners who shall be exclusively responsible in 
the territories within their jurisdiction for the assessment and 
collection of the duty of excise levied under clause (b) of 
section 2 of the Iron Ore Mines Labour Welfare Cess Act, 
1961 (58 of 1961), and accured due before the 1st day of 
October, 1974.
